        Idea:
Add a final video available marker to shot groups in the video editor timeline. When a shot group has a final video (from shot_final_videos table), show a video icon badge on the right side of the shot group overlay (similar to stale variant amber badge). Clicking opens a panel with: 1) Switch to final video - removes all image clips in that shot group and replaces with a single final video clip spanning the same time range, 2) Dismiss reminder - hides the marker. Follow existing patterns: stale variant marker (useStaleVariants.ts, ClipAction.tsx), shot groups (useShotGroups.ts, TimelineCanvas.tsx), final video data (useShotFinalVideos.ts), asset registration (useAssetManagement.ts). Key replacement logic: register final video as asset, calculate time span of shot group clips, remove those clips, insert single final video clip, persist changes.

User notes and answers:
- Addressing all flags: (1) Pass full group identity (clipIds + rowId) through callbacks, not just shotId. Badge click and switch action receive the PositionedShotGroup. (2) Dont use registerGenerationAsset - directly create AssetRegistryEntry with type:video/mp4 and call patchRegistry+registerAsset like useStaleVariants does. (3) Clip spans shot group time range, no separate duration fetch needed. (4) Extend existing shotGroupMenu state to include clipIds and hasFinalVideo, add Switch to Final Video to existing context menu. Keep badge but have it trigger same menu. (5) Use context menu matching existing pattern. (6) Only show on 2+ clip groups. (7) Skip unit tests for wiring - TypeScript compilation is sufficient.

}

        Idea:
Add a final video available marker to shot groups in the video editor timeline. When a shot group has a final video (from shot_final_videos table), show a video icon badge on the right side of the shot group overlay (similar to stale variant amber badge). Clicking opens a panel with: 1) Switch to final video - removes all image clips in that shot group and replaces with a single final video clip spanning the same time range, 2) Dismiss reminder - hides the marker. Follow existing patterns: stale variant marker (useStaleVariants.ts, ClipAction.tsx), shot groups (useShotGroups.ts, TimelineCanvas.tsx), final video data (useShotFinalVideos.ts), asset registration (useAssetManagement.ts). Key replacement logic: register final video as asset, calculate time span of shot group clips, remove those clips, insert single final video clip, persist changes.

User notes and answers:
- Addressing all flags: (1) Pass full group identity (clipIds + rowId) through callbacks, not just shotId. Badge click and switch action receive the PositionedShotGroup. (2) Dont use registerGenerationAsset - directly create AssetRegistryEntry with type:video/mp4 and call patchRegistry+registerAsset like useStaleVariants does. (3) Clip spans shot group time range, no separate duration fetch needed. (4) Extend existing shotGroupMenu state to include clipIds and hasFinalVideo, add Switch to Final Video to existing context menu. Keep badge but have it trigger same menu. (5) Use context menu matching existing pattern. (6) Only show on 2+ clip groups. (7) Skip unit tests for wiring - TypeScript compilation is sufficient.

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-tool-utils: agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

        Idea:
Add a final video available marker to shot groups in the video editor timeline. When a shot group has a final video (from shot_final_videos table), show a video icon badge on the right side of the shot group overlay (similar to stale variant amber badge). Clicking opens a panel with: 1) Switch to final video - removes all image clips in that shot group and replaces with a single final video clip spanning the same time range, 2) Dismiss reminder - hides the marker. Follow existing patterns: stale variant marker (useStaleVariants.ts, ClipAction.tsx), shot groups (useShotGroups.ts, TimelineCanvas.tsx), final video data (useShotFinalVideos.ts), asset registration (useAssetManagement.ts). Key replacement logic: register final video as asset, calculate time span of shot group clips, remove those clips, insert single final video clip, persist changes.

User notes and answers:
- Addressing all flags: (1) Pass full group identity (clipIds + rowId) through callbacks, not just shotId. Badge click and switch action receive the PositionedShotGroup. (2) Dont use registerGenerationAsset - directly create AssetRegistryEntry with type:video/mp4 and call patchRegistry+registerAsset like useStaleVariants does. (3) Clip spans shot group time range, no separate duration fetch needed. (4) Extend existing shotGroupMenu state to include clipIds and hasFinalVideo, add Switch to Final Video to existing context menu. Keep badge but have it trigger same menu. (5) Use context menu matching existing pattern. (6) Only show on 2+ clip groups. (7) Skip unit tests for wiring - TypeScript compilation is sufficient.

(D) Implement `handleSwitchToFinalVideo` callback taking `{ shotId, clipIds, rowId }`: (1) get finalVideo from finalVideoMap, get current from dataRef.current, bail if missing; (2) register asset: `const assetKey = generateUUID()`, create `AssetRegistryEntry` with `{ file: finalVideo.location, type: 'video/mp4', generationId: finalVideo.id }`, call `patchRegistry(assetKey, entry, finalVideo.location)` then `void registerAsset(assetKey, entry).catch(console.error)`; (3) calculate time span: iterate current.rows actions, find min start/max end for clipIds; (4) build new clip: `const clipId = getNextClipId(current.meta)`, create ClipMeta with `{ asset: assetKey, track: rowId, clipType: 'media', from: 0, to: endTime - startTime, speed: 1, volume: 1, opacity: 1 }`, create TimelineAction with `{ id: clipId, start: startTime, end: endTime, effectId: 'effect-' + clipId }`; (5) build nextRows: map rows, filter out old clipIds from actions, add videoAction to the row matching rowId; (6) call `applyEdit({ type: 'rows', rows: nextRows, metaUpdates: { [clipId]: videoMeta }, metaDeletes: clipIds })`. (E) Pass new props to TimelineCanvas at line 497-531: `finalVideoShotIds`, `onShotGroupSwitchToFinalVideo={handleSwitchToFinalVideo}`, `onShotGroupDismissFinalVideo={dismissShot}`.",

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Add a final video available marker to shot groups in the video editor timeline. When a shot group has a final video (from shot_final_videos table), show a video icon badge on the right side of the shot group overlay (similar to stale variant amber badge). Clicking opens a panel with: 1) Switch to final video - removes all image clips in that shot group and replaces with a single final video clip spanning the same time range, 2) Dismiss reminder - hides the marker. Follow existing patterns: stale variant marker (useStaleVariants.ts, ClipAction.tsx), shot groups (useShotGroups.ts, TimelineCanvas.tsx), final video data (useShotFinalVideos.ts), asset registration (useAssetManagement.ts). Key replacement logic: register final video as asset, calculate time span of shot group clips, remove those clips, insert single final video clip, persist changes.

User notes and answers:
- Addressing all flags: (1) Pass full group identity (clipIds + rowId) through callbacks, not just shotId. Badge click and switch action receive the PositionedShotGroup. (2) Dont use registerGenerationAsset - directly create AssetRegistryEntry with type:video/mp4 and call patchRegistry+registerAsset like useStaleVariants does. (3) Clip spans shot group time range, no separate duration fetch needed. (4) Extend existing shotGroupMenu state to include clipIds and hasFinalVideo, add Switch to Final Video to existing context menu. Keep badge but have it trigger same menu. (5) Use context menu matching existing pattern. (6) Only show on 2+ clip groups. (7) Skip unit tests for wiring - TypeScript compilation is sufficient.

    "FLAG-001 (resolved): Badge must have `pointerEvents: 'auto'` since the shot group border div is `pointer-events-none`. The badge is a SIBLING element, not a child of the border div.",
    "FLAG-002 (resolved): Asset registration must use direct `AssetRegistryEntry` with `type: 'video/mp4'` + `patchRegistry` + `registerAsset`. Do NOT use `registerGenerationAsset`.",
    "correctness-1/correctness-3 (accepted): Final video clip is sized to shot group time span (from:0, to:duration). If video is shorter than span, it holds last frame. This is accepted behavior for v1.",
    "correctness-2/scope (accepted): Deliberately bypasses useAssetManagement insertion path. The switch action needs atomic remove+insert in a single applyEdit call. Manual-track geometry defaults and clipOrder are handled by applyEdit's internal wiring.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

useSwitchToFinalVideo({\n+  applyEdit,\n+  dataRef,\n+  finalVideoMap,\n+  patchRegistry,\n+  registerAsset,\n+}: UseSwitchToFinalVideoArgs) {\n+  return useCallback(({ shotId, clipIds, rowId }: { === 0 || !current.rows.some((row) => row.id === rowId)) {\n+      return;\n+    }\n+\n+    const clipIdSet = new Set(clipIds);\n+    let startTime = Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY;\n+    let endTime = startTime = Math.min(startTime, action.start);\n+          endTime = Math.max(endTime, action.end);\n+          matchedClipCount += 1;\n+        }\n+      }\n+    }\n+    if (!Number.isFinite(startTime) || !Number.isFinite(endTime) || matchedClipCount !== clipIdSet.size || endTime <= startTime) {\n+      return;\n+    }\n+\n+    const assetKey = generateUUID();\n+    const assetEntry: AssetRegistryEntry = { file: finalVideo.location, type: 'video/mp4', generationId: finalVideo.id };\n+    patchRegistry(assetKey, assetEntry, finalVideo.location);\n+    void registerAsset(assetKey, assetEntry).catch((error) => {\n+      console.error('[TimelineEditor] Failed to persist final video asset:', error);\n+    });\n+\n+    const clipId = 'manual';\n+    const videoMeta: ClipMeta = {\n+      asset: assetKey,\n+      track: rowId,\n+      clipType: 'media',\n+      from: 0,\n+      to: endTime - startTime,\n+      speed: 1,\n+      volume: 1,\n+      opacity: 1,\n+      x: isManualVisualTrack ? 100 : undefined,\n+      y: isManualVisualTrack ? 100 : undefined,\n+      width: isManualVisualTrack ? 320 : undefined,\n+      height: isManualVisualTrack ? 240 : undefined,\n+    };\n+    const videoAction: TimelineAction = { id: clipId, start: startTime, end: endTime, effectId: `effect-${clipId}` };\n+\n+    const nextRows = current.rows.map((row) => {\n+      const remainingActions = row.actions.filter((action) => !clipIdSet.has(action.id));\n+      if (row.id !== rowId) {\n+        return remainingActions.length === row.actions.length ? row : { ...row, actions: remainingActions };\n+      }\n+      const insertionIndex = row.actions.findIndex((action) => clipIdSet.has(action.id));\n+      const nextActions = insertionIndex < 0\n+        ? [...remainingActions, videoAction]\n+        : [...remainingActions.slice(0, insertionIndex), videoAction, ...remainingActions.slice(insertionIndex)];\n+      return { ...row, actions: nextActions };\n+    });\n+    const nextClipOrder = updateClipOrder(current.clipOrder, rowId, (ids) => {\n+      const filtered = ids.filter((id) => !clipIdSet.has(id));\n+      const insertionIndex = ids.findIndex((id) => clipIdSet.has(id));\n+      return insertionIndex < 0\n+        ?
